<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We resolve the almost-everywhere regularity conjecture of Brena, Decio, and De Lellis for stationary integral varifolds of arbitrary positive dimension and codimension in Euclidean open sets. The singular set has zero measure in the dimension of the varifold: near almost every support point, the varifold is a constant positive integer multiple of a smooth embedded minimal submanifold. The corresponding statement also holds on round spheres.

- **claim-main-regularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Every stationary integral $m$-varifold in an open subset of $\mathbb R^{m+n}$ satisfies $$\mathcal H^m(\operatorname{Sing}V)=0.$$ Here $m,n\geq1$ are arbitrary. No stability, minimizing, orientability, density upper bound, or codimension-one hypothesis is required.
  - Explanation: Every stationary integral varifold of positive dimension and codimension in an open Euclidean set has a singular set of zero measure in the dimension of the varifold, with no extra stability or density hypothesis.
- **claim-round-spheres** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-round-spheres)): Let $m,n\geq1$ and let $V$ be an integral $m$-varifold on the full unit round sphere $S^{m+n}$, stationary for variations tangent to the sphere. A support point is regular if on a spherical neighborhood $V=q\|\Sigma\|$ for an integer $q\geq1$ and a smooth embedded minimal $m$-submanifold $\Sigma$ of the sphere. If $\operatorname{Sing}_S V$ denotes the remaining support points, then $$\mathcal H^m_{g_{\mathrm{round}}}(\operatorname{Sing}_S V)=0.$$ No fixed upper bound on mass, density, or multiplicity is required, nor are stability, orientability, or mod-two cyclicity. The same conclusion holds on every round sphere of positive radius.
  - Explanation: The same measure-zero conclusion holds for integral varifolds on a round sphere that are stationary for variations tangent to the sphere.
- **claim-monotonicity-mean-value** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lt-monotonicity)): Let $V$ be stationary and integral in an open Euclidean set. If $p\in\mathop{\mathrm{spt}}\mu$ and $\overline{\mathbf B_r(p)}$ lies in that set, then $$\label{eq:lt-lower-mass} \mu(\mathbf B_r(p))\ge\omega_m r^m.$$ If $u\ge0$ is a $C^2$ ambient function with $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(P_TD^2u)\ge0$ for $V$-almost every $(x,T)$ in the ball, then $$\label{eq:lt-mean-value} u(p)\le \frac{1}{\omega_m r^m} \int_{\mathbf B_r(p)}u\,\mathop{}\!d\mu.$$ The same assertion holds for continuous functions satisfying the corresponding weak subharmonicity inequality.
  - Explanation: Stationarity gives a lower mass bound at every support point and a mean-value inequality for nonnegative subharmonic functions.
- **claim-curved-first-variation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lt-curvedvariation)): For a compactly supported tangent field $X$ on $S$, $$\label{eq:lt-curvedvariation} \begin{split} &\left\|\int\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_SX\,\mathop{}\!dM-\int B:\nabla^SX\,\mathop{}\!d\mu\right\|\\ &\quad\le C\int\Big\{\|z_{\rm phys}\|e\|\nabla^SX\| +\big[(\|\nabla^\perp H_S\|+\|z_{\rm phys}\|)\|z_{\rm phys}\| +\sqrt e\,\|z_{\rm phys}\|\big]\|X\|\Big\}\mathop{}\!d\mu. \end{split}$$ The integrands containing tensors are evaluated at $q$, with the base field pulled back by $\pi$. In coordinates scaled by $l$, the right side is bounded by $$\label{eq:lt-scaledvariation} C\gamma\int(e+\lambda)(\|\nabla X\|+\|X\|)\,\mathop{}\!d\mu.$$ Here and below a dimensionless scaled test field is used in a scaled identity.
  - Explanation: The horizontal first variation over a curved reference surface is compared with the tilt term up to errors from height, tilt and curvature.
- **claim-projection-integer-count** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lt-jacobian)): The tangential Jacobian of $\pi$ is $$\label{eq:lt-jacobian} J_\pi=\frac{\sqrt{\det P_{hh}}}{\det J_z},\qquad \|J_\pi-1\|\le C(e+\lambda).$$ There is a nonnegative integer-valued function $N$ on the base, finite almost everywhere, such that $$\label{eq:lt-integer-measure} \pi_\#(J_\pi\mu)=N\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_S, \qquad \|\nu-N\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_S\|\le C(D+\Lambda).$$ The inequalities are measure inequalities on every relatively compact base set. Moreover, for sufficiently small $e$, $$\label{eq:lt-signed-jacobian} 2(1-J_\pi)-e\le Ce^2+C(\|z_{\rm phys}\|^2+\|H_S\|^2).$$
  - Explanation: The projection Jacobian is close to one and the projected measure is an integer count times base volume up to tilt and curvature errors.
- **claim-scalar-potential** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lt-potential)): Suppose a finite scalar measure $v$ on $B_4\subset\mathbb R^m$ satisfies $$\label{eq:lt-gradient-equation} \partial_jv=\sum_i\partial_i F_{ij}+g_j, \qquad \sum_{ij}\|F_{ij}\|(B_4)+\sum_j\|g_j\|(B_4)\le\epsilon.$$ Then there is a real constant $c$ such that the density $v_{\rm ac}$ obeys $$\label{eq:lt-weak-one} \|\{y\in B_2:\|v_{\rm ac}(y)-c\|>t\}\| \le C\epsilon/t\quad(t>0),$$ and, for every smooth test supported in $B_2$, $$\label{eq:lt-distribution-control} \|\langle v-c\,\mathop{}\!dy,\varphi\rangle\| \le C\epsilon\\|\varphi\\|_{C^{k}},$$ where $k$ is a fixed integer depending only on $m$. Fixed changes of the radii give the same conclusion.
  - Explanation: A scalar measure whose gradient is small divergence-form data is, modulo a constant, controlled in weak L1 and in a distributional norm.
- **claim-scalar-mass-integer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lt-scalar)): Fix a bound for $Q$ and fixed concentric interior enlargements of a ball $B_s$. Suppose the normalized projected mass on the outer enlargement is bounded, and $$\epsilon=s^{-m}(D+\Lambda)(B_{cs})$$ is sufficiently small; $c>1$ is a fixed enlargement with room for the argument. The count selects a unique integer $q\ge0$ on most of an intermediate ball. If either a fixed smooth mass average differs from $Q$ by less than $1/4$, or $N=Q$ on a fixed majority of that ball, then $q=Q$. In that case $$\label{eq:lt-scalar-mass} \|M\|(B_s)+\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_S\{y\in B_s:N(y)\ne Q\} \le C(D+\Lambda)(B_{cs}).$$ In the smooth-average condition the test is nonnegative, has reference integral one, and has fixed scaled derivative bounds. The same integer propagates along overlapping comparable balls for which these small-energy and mass bounds hold and whose overlap has a fixed positive relative volume.
  - Explanation: Small excess selects a unique integer count, identified with Q by a smooth-average or majority condition, with mass control and propagation along overlapping balls.
- **claim-configuration-heights** (construction, [source](#stmt-lt-config)): Fix $Q\ge1$, and choose a sufficiently large fixed enlargement $c>1$ depending only on the dimensions, $Q$, and the fixed coordinate and cylinder-room data. For every prescribed exceptional proportion $\delta_0>0$ there is a threshold $d_0=d_0(\delta_0)>0$, depending otherwise only on these fixed data, such that the following holds. Suppose $Q$ is selected on the enlarged ball $B_{cs}(x)$ and $$s^{-m}\tau(B_{cs}(x))\le d\le d_0,$$ with the cylinder and interior-room conventions above. There is a configuration $\mathcal A=\{a_1,\ldots,a_Q\}$ of scaled heights such that, outside a subset of $B_s(x)$ of relative volume at most $\delta_0$, the fiber consists of exactly $Q$ projection entries and can be matched to $\mathcal A$ within $C_{\delta_0}s\sqrt d$. Configurations on comparable overlapping balls can be chosen, and any such majority configurations can be matched, within the sum of these bounds, provided the exceptional proportions are smaller than a fixed fraction of the overlap.* *For every fixed finite $p>2$ satisfying $$\label{eq:lt-p-range} m(1/2-1/p)<1,$$ and every fixed $0<\beta<\min\{1/2,1/m\}$, this same configuration satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \left(s^{-m}\int_{\pi^{-1}(B_s(x))} \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(\zeta,\mathcal A)^p\,\mathop{}\!d\mu\right)^{1/p} &\le C_p s\sqrt d,\label{eq:lt-config-moment}\\ \sup_{\mathop{\mathrm{spt}}\mu\cap\pi^{-1}(B_s(x))} \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(\zeta,\mathcal A)&\le C_\beta s d^\beta. \label{eq:lt-config-sup} \end{aligned}$$ The enlargement $c$, threshold $d_0$, and configuration do not depend on $p$ or $\beta$. The constants $C_p,C_\beta$ may depend on their indicated exponents and the fixed data, but not on $\delta_0$; the majority constant $C_{\delta_0}$ may depend on it. The support estimate holds for all support points, including points with zero projection Jacobian.
  - Explanation: Under small excess there is a configuration of Q heights matching most fibers, with moment bounds and a support-height bound.
- **claim-gaussian-weighted-balls** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lt-weightedballs)): Define $$G_s(y)=(2\pi s^2)^{-m/2}\exp(-\|y\|^2/(2s^2))$$ and fix a center $x$. On the region $\|y-x\|\le Rs$, cover the base by balls of radii comparable to $s/(1+\|y-x\|/s)$, with fixed enlarged balls having bounded overlap. Suppose each enlarged ball meets the hypotheses of [lt:scalar](#lt:scalar) with the same propagated integer $Q$. Then $$\label{eq:lt-weighted-scalar} \int_{\|y-x\|\le Rs}G_s(y-x)\,\mathop{}\!d\|M\|(y) \le C\int_{\|y-x\|\le (R+C)s}G_s(y-x)\,\mathop{}\!d(D+\Lambda)(y).$$ The constant is independent of $R$. The part outside the indicated region is retained as a tail error.* *For the configuration conclusion, fix one exceptional proportion $\delta_{\rm cmp}>0$ smaller than the fixed comparison-overlap proportions. If, in addition, on all admissible dilations $1\le t\le R_*$ one has $$\label{eq:lt-polynomial-dilation} (ts)^{-m}\tau(B_{cts}(x))\le C t^Jd,$$ with $Ct^Jd\le d_0(\delta_{\rm cmp})$ throughout this range and the same integer propagated, then configurations matched from the scale $s$ have moment and support estimates on a dilation $t$ equal to those in [lt:config](#lt:config), with an additional fixed power of $t$. Gaussian integrals of these estimates have constants depending on $J$ and the fixed moment order, once the tail beyond $R_*$ is accounted for.
  - Explanation: Scalar mass control and configuration bounds sum over bounded-overlap covers with Gaussian weights and extend to polynomial dilations.
- **claim-gaussian-mass-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lt-gaussian)): Set $u=(y-x)/s$, $A(s)=s^2M_s(x)$, and $$R_s(x)=\int G_s(y-x)B[u,u]\,\mathop{}\!d\mu.$$ In flat coordinates, apart from $\mathcal T_s$, $$\label{eq:lt-gaussian-flat} \dot A=s^2L_s+s^2R_s, \qquad \dot{}=\frac{\mathop{}\!d}{\mathop{}\!d\log s}.$$ In the curved coordinates fixed above, $$\label{eq:lt-gaussian-curved} \dot A=s^2L_s+s^2R_s+\mathcal E_s+\mathcal T_s, \qquad \|\mathcal E_s\|\le C\gamma s^2 \int G_s(y-x)(1+\|u\|^2)\,\mathop{}\!d\tau(y).$$ Here the entries of $u$ in $B[u,u]$ are taken in the chosen orthonormal horizontal frame, and $\gamma$ retains its definition from the fixed physical coordinates.
  - Explanation: The scale derivative of the Gaussian-averaged mass excess splits into the signed source, a radial tilt term, geometric error and chart tails.
- **claim-height-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lt-heighttests)): In flat coordinates, for a base test $\varphi$, a scalar height function $f$, a horizontal field $X$, and a normal vector function $v$, stationarity gives $$\begin{aligned} \int f\mathop{\mathrm{div}}X\,\mathop{}\!d\mu &=\int f B:DX\,\mathop{}\!d\mu- \int Df\cdot P_{vh}X\,\mathop{}\!d\mu, \label{eq:lt-height-horizontal}\\ 0&=\int\{\varphi Dv:P_{vv} +v\cdot P_{vh}\nabla\varphi\}\,\mathop{}\!d\mu. \label{eq:lt-height-normal} \end{aligned}$$ All height functions here use the scaled variable $\zeta$. For a scale-independent $C^2$ height function $\Gamma$, these formulas give $$\label{eq:lt-height-gaussian} \frac{\mathop{}\!d}{\mathop{}\!d\log s}\int G_s\Gamma\,\mathop{}\!d\mu =\int G_s\Gamma(B[u,u]-e)\,\mathop{}\!d\mu +s^2\int G_sD^2\Gamma:P_{vv}\,\mathop{}\!d\mu,$$ apart from the specified chart tails. If $\Gamma$ depends on $s$, add $\int G_s\dot\Gamma\,\mathop{}\!d\mu$.* *In the curved tube, use the original radial-gauge normal frame fixed above. The extra trace terms in a normal test $\varphi v(\zeta)$ without a derivative of $\varphi$ are bounded pointwise by $C\|\varphi\|$ times $$\label{eq:lt-normal-error} \|Dv\|l\|z_{\rm phys}\|\sqrt e +l\|v\|\bigl(\|H_S\|+\|z_{\rm phys}\|+e+\sqrt e\bigr).$$ The terms with a derivative of $\varphi$ have the flat tensor expression in <a href="#eq:lt-height-normal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lt-height-normal">[eq:lt-height-normal]</a>, with a coefficient error at most $C\gamma\|P_{vh}\|$. The curved horizontal identity is covariant, with $\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_SX$ and $\nabla^SX$ in place of the flat base operators. Its geometric error is bounded by the right side of <a href="#eq:lt-scaledvariation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lt-scaledvariation">[eq:lt-scaledvariation]</a> with the integrand multiplied by $\|f\|$. The additional flux error from the height gradient is bounded pointwise by $$\label{eq:lt-horizontal-height-error} C\gamma \|Df\|\|X\|(\sqrt e+\sqrt\lambda).$$ In its coordinate form the divergence comparison also contributes an error bounded by $Cl\int\|f\|\|X\|\,\mathop{}\!d\mu$; the coordinate changes in the $B$ term are included in the preceding geometric error. Under a further interior rescaling, retain the original horizontal and normal frames and the original $\gamma$ in <a href="#eq:lt-small-geometry" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lt-small-geometry">[eq:lt-small-geometry]</a>. Transform the displayed estimates by change of variables. The inherited physical connection remains $O(l)$; no improvement to $O(ls)$ is asserted on an off-center ball of coordinate radius $s$. In particular first-order flux errors have size at most $C\gamma(\sqrt e+\sqrt\lambda)$ times the relevant multiplier, whereas quadratic errors have size at most $C\gamma(e+\lambda)$. The factor $\|v\|$ in <a href="#eq:lt-normal-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lt-normal-error">[eq:lt-normal-error]</a> must be retained when estimating a normal test.
  - Explanation: Stationarity tested with height-dependent fields gives identities for height integrals and their Gaussian scale derivatives, with geometric error bounds.
- **claim-signed-excess** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sx-signed)): Fix a positive integer $Q$ and $\kappa>0$. Work in the interior normal-cylinder setting of [sec:local](#sec:local), with base coordinates scaled by a physical radius $l$ so that $B_4$ is available. Suppose $$\label{eq:signed-smallness} \tau(B_4)\leq d=e^{-T}, \qquad l+\sup\|z_{\mathrm{phys}}\|\leq e^{-\kappa T}.$$ The supremum is over the restricted support above the available chart. The statement also holds after an interior rescaling in the inherited frames of [lt:heighttests](#lt:heighttests), provided in the curved case the second smallness condition is imposed on the inherited parameter $\gamma$ from <a href="#eq:lt-small-geometry" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lt-small-geometry">[eq:lt-small-geometry]</a>. There are a constant $C_Q$ and a threshold $T_0$, depending only on the stated fixed data, such that for $T\geq T_0$, $$\label{eq:signed-conclusion} \int w\,(L-C_Q\Lambda)\leq e^{-F_Q(T)}d$$ whenever $w$ is supported in $B_1$, $0\leq w\leq1$, and $\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}w\leq1$. For a flat reference plane, take $\Lambda=0$ and omit the second condition in <a href="#eq:signed-smallness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:signed-smallness">[eq:signed-smallness]</a>; constant sheet offsets need not be bounded.
  - Explanation: The weighted signed excess minus a curvature correction is bounded by an iterated-exponential gain times the small total excess.
- **claim-contact-measure** (construction, [source](#stmt-ct-contact)): Let $f$ be a finite signed Borel measure on $X$, with $M_f=f(X)>0$, and let $K$ be the entrance boundary of a continuation set. There is a probability measure $\sigma$ on $K$ such that the positive spatial measure $$h=M_f(\pi_X)_\#\sigma$$ has mass $M_f$ and, at every $(x,s_0)\in\mathop{\mathrm{spt}}\sigma$, $$\label{ct:contact-signs} (f-h)_{s_0}(x)\ge0, \qquad \int_{s_0}^{s}v^2(f-h)_v(x)\,\mathop{}\!d\log v\ge0 \quad(s\ge s_0).$$ The second inequality concerns the integral; it does not assert a pointwise sign at scales larger than $s_0$.
  - Explanation: A heat-potential fixed-point construction gives a probability measure on the entrance boundary with an initial and a cumulative sign at each contact.
- **claim-weight-localization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ct-localization)): Let $\|L'\|\le C_0\tau$, where $\tau$ is a finite positive measure on a torus of diameter comparable to $R$, and let $0\le w\le1$, $\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}w\le R^{-1}$. Put $$\nu=\tau+d\,\mathop{}\!dy,\qquad f=wL'-\eta\nu, \qquad 0<\eta<1.$$ Suppose, for some fixed $C_1$, that $\tau(X)/(dR^m)\le e^{C_1T}$. For all sufficiently large $T$, if $$\label{ct:localization-scale} 0<\rho\le c\eta^2R/T^2,$$ then, uniformly for every $x\in X$ and $0<s\le\rho$, $$\label{ct:localized-source} f_s(x)\le w(x)L'_s(x)-\frac\eta2\bigl(\tau_s(x)+d\bigr).$$ Here $c>0$ depends only on $C_0,C_1$ and the fixed torus shape. If $\sigma,h$ are furnished by [ct:contact](#ct:contact) with contacts at scales $s_0\le\rho$, then $w(x)>0$ at each contact, and $$\begin{aligned} L'_{s_0}(x)&\ge h_{s_0}(x)+\tfrac\eta2\nu_{s_0}(x), \label{ct:localized-initial}\\ \int_{s_0}^s v^2\bigl(L'-\tfrac\eta2\nu\bigr)_v(x) \,\mathop{}\!d\log v&\ge0\qquad(s_0\le s\le\rho). \label{ct:localized-cumulative} \end{aligned}$$ In particular, contacts lie in the interior region containing $\{w>0\}$. These conclusions are uniform as the bottom scale tends to zero.
  - Explanation: After subtracting a small margin and restricting to small outer scales, contacts lie where the weight is positive and both sign conditions hold.
- **claim-separated-configurations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ct-separated)): Fix $Q>1$ and assume [sx:signed](#sx:signed) for every integer $1\le q<Q$. For each fixed $P>0$ there are constants $C_Q,C_S$ and a function $b=b_Q$ satisfying $$\label{ct:b-choice} b(T)\gg(\log T)^{200},\qquad b(T)=T^{o(1)},\qquad F_{Q-1}(b(T)/2)\ge(\log T)^3$$ such that the following holds. Under the hypotheses of [sx:signed](#sx:signed), let $\mathcal A=\{a_1,\ldots,a_Q\}$ be any majority configuration on $B_{r_{\rm maj}}$ with local upper bound $d_{\rm loc}=r_{\rm maj}^{-m}d$, and suppose it has a partition into at least two nonempty groups separated by $10S(T)\sqrt d$, where $$S(T)=e^{C_Sb(T)},\qquad c(T)=\frac{(\log T)^2}{T}.$$ Let $D_{\rm far}$ be the projection of tilt restricted to $\{\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(z,\mathcal A)>S(T)\sqrt d\}$. Then every nonnegative weight $w\le1$, supported in $B_1$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}w\le1$, satisfies $$\label{ct:separated-estimate} \int w\,\mathop{}\!d\bigl(L-C_Q\Lambda+c(T)D_{\rm far}\bigr) \le T^{-P}d.$$ The statement holds on other fixed interior balls after rescaling. Polynomial factors in the Lipschitz norm or the enlargement of a Gaussian working ball can be allowed by increasing $P,C_S$ and the fixed constants.
  - Explanation: Assuming the signed estimate at smaller integers, a well-separated majority configuration gives a polynomially small bound plus a gain from far tilt.
- **claim-bottom-contribution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ex-bottom)): The mass $\widehat h_\sigma(K_\sigma\setminus\mathcal G_\sigma)$ is at most $$o_{\sigma\downarrow0}(1)+Cd_*d.$$ Every contact in $\mathcal G_\sigma$ has a scale $s_0\in[\underline s,\rho)$ with $\tau_{s_0}(x)=d_*$. The inequalities <a href="#ex:threshold-control" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ex:threshold-control">[ex:threshold-control]</a> hold also when $\underline s<s_0$.
  - Explanation: Bottom contacts without a threshold equality carry vanishing mass as the bottom scale tends to zero, up to a small multiple of the threshold density.
- **claim-future-envelope** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ex-envelope)): Set $$I(s)=\max\left\{ \sup_{s\le v\le\rho}(s/v)^K A(v),\, (s/\rho)^K\rho^2d\rho^{-2m-4}\right\}, \qquad a(s)=\log\frac{s^2}{I(s)}. \label{ex:future}$$ Starting at a threshold scale $s_0$, stop at the first scale $s_*$ with $a(s_*)=T/2$. This scale exists, and on the path $[s_0,s_*]$ one has $$a(s_0)=a_0+O(1),\qquad a(s)\ge a_0-O(1),\qquad v^2\tau_v\le C(v/s)^K I(s)\quad(s\le v\le\rho). \label{ex:dilations}$$ The functions $I,a$ are locally Lipschitz in $\log s$. Almost everywhere they satisfy $$\begin{cases} I=A,\quad \dot I=\dot A,&\text{on the reset set }\{I=A\},\\ \dot I=KI,&\text{on its complement}, \end{cases} \qquad \|\dot a\|+\dfrac{\|\dot I\|+\|\dot Z\|}{I}\le C. \label{ex:derivatives}$$ Moreover $\dot Z/I$ is locally Lipschitz, with derivative bounded by $C$. For every fixed $P$, weighted scalar control gives $$\|M\|_s\le C(D_s+\Lambda_s)+a^{-P}I(s)/s^2. \label{ex:weighted-scalar}$$ All constants are uniform in the contact and the bottom.
  - Explanation: A positive envelope of future signed mass controls unsigned excess averages at larger scales and satisfies reset and derivative rules along a path.
- **claim-barrier-initialization** (construction, [source](#stmt-ex-initialization)): At $s_0$ one can choose at most $Q$ wells and a small parameter $k_0>0$ so that the barrier of [ex:barrier](#ex:barrier) holds initially. If more than one well is used, one may arrange, for any fixed $P$, $$Z(s_0)\le a_0^{-P}I(s_0),\qquad U(s_0)\le CI(s_0),$$ before any immediate mergers, with $k_0$ a small fixed constant. Alternatively there is one common well, with $$U(s_0)\le CS_h(a_0)^2 I(s_0),\qquad I(s_0)-Z(s_0)\ge cS_h(a_0)^{-2}I(s_0), \qquad k_0=cS_h(a_0)^{-6}.$$ For $Q=1$, $k_0$ is a small fixed constant.
  - Explanation: At the threshold scale one selects at most Q wells and a small parameter so that the barrier holds initially.
- **claim-low-frequency-barrier** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ex-barrier)): Along every path in [ex:envelope](#ex:envelope), the wells can be chosen piecewise constantly, with at most $Q-1$ mergers. There is a positive piecewise smooth parameter $k$, bounded above by a small fixed constant and satisfying between mergers $$\frac{k_a}{k}=q=-\frac{k}{a^2}, \label{ex:k-evolution}$$ such that the comparison <a href="#ex:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ex:comparison">[ex:comparison]</a> holds. Here $C_1$ is fixed and sufficiently large. Uniformly along the path, $\log(1/k)=o(a_0)$ as $T\to\infty$. Before any hypothetical first failure of the barrier, the following estimates hold, with $\mathcal E\le a^{-P_0/2}$: $$\begin{aligned} \dot U&\le2s^2D_s+Cs^2D_{\mathrm{tr}}+\mathcal E I, \label{ex:moment-derivative}\\ s^2R_s&\ge4k(s^2D_s-Cs^2D_{\mathrm{tr}}) -4k^2U-\mathcal E kI. \label{ex:radial-bound} \end{aligned}$$ With more than one well, in addition, $$\dot Z+c_2\frac{(\log a)^2}{a}s^2D_{\mathrm{tr}} \le a^{-P_0}I. \label{ex:transition}$$ The transition terms are absent with one well. By increasing the fixed exponents in the construction, $\mathcal E$ can be smaller than any prescribed inverse power of $a$.
  - Explanation: Along every envelope path the wells can be chosen piecewise constantly with finitely many mergers so that the moment comparison is maintained.
- **claim-band-choice** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ex-bands)): Fix $\varepsilon_0>0$, sufficiently small for the later packing argument, and any fixed loss exponent $C_*$. For large $T$, there are $Q$ ordered disjoint bands $[B_i,2B_i]\subset(2a_0,T/2)$, independent of the contact, such that $$B_1^{1/10}\gg F_Q(T)^3,\qquad S_h(a_0)^{C_*}\ll B_1^{\varepsilon_0},\qquad S_1(2B_i)^{C_*}\ll B_{i+1}^{\varepsilon_0}. \label{ex:band-spacing}$$ For $Q=1$, only the first condition is needed, and a single band of size comparable to $T$ suffices. Every contact path has a traversal contained in one band, from $a=B$ to $a=2B$, which contains no merger and on which $k\ge B^{-\varepsilon_0}$. The estimates <a href="#ex:bank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ex:bank">[ex:bank]</a>, <a href="#ex:dilations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ex:dilations">[ex:dilations]</a>, <a href="#ex:derivatives" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ex:derivatives">[ex:derivatives]</a>, <a href="#ex:weighted-scalar" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ex:weighted-scalar">[ex:weighted-scalar]</a>, and <a href="#ex:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ex:comparison">[ex:comparison]</a>–<a href="#ex:transition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ex:transition">[ex:transition]</a> hold on that traversal. If it has several wells, the bound $\dot Z\le a^{-P_0}I$ holds on every preceding stage with several wells as well, and the initial value of $Z$ is the arbitrarily small quantity in [ex:initialization](#ex:initialization).
  - Explanation: There are ordered disjoint level bands such that every contact path has a merger-free traversal inside one band with the coefficient bounded below.
- **claim-ordered-witnesses** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pk-witnesses)): Consider a contact path and a merger-free traversal of $[B,2B]$ provided by [ex:bands](#ex:bands), with $k\ge B^{-\varepsilon_0}$ throughout the traversal. Given a sufficiently small fixed number $\vartheta>0$, there are at least $cB^{1/2}$ scales $s_j$, ordered in outward time, whose levels satisfy $$\label{pk:level-spacing} B+1<a(s_j)<2B-1,\qquad a(s_{j+1})-a(s_j)\ge B^{1/3}.$$ If more than one well remains, these scales satisfy $$\label{pk:multi-witness} D_{s_j}\ge c e^{-a(s_j)},\qquad D_{\mathrm{tr}}(s_j)\le\vartheta e^{-a(s_j)}.$$ If only one well remains, they satisfy $$\label{pk:single-witness} D_{s_j}\ge B^{-\varepsilon_0}e^{-a(s_j)}.$$ The constants are independent of the contact and of the positive bottom scale. The large threshold for $B$ may depend on $\varepsilon_0$.
  - Explanation: A merger-free traversal contains many well-spaced scales with a definite tilt and, with several wells, small transition tilt.
- **claim-controlled-density-fraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pk-reverse)): Fix a finite family of nested dyadic grids with the property that every sufficiently small ball is contained in a cube of one of the grids with comparable diameter. The grids may be taken half-open, so their cubes form genuine partitions. At each witness of [pk:witnesses](#pk:witnesses) there is a cube $J$ of one of these grids such that, with $\delta=e^{-a(s)}$, $$\label{pk:cube-range} B_s(x)\subset\operatorname{int}J,\qquad \ell(J)\le C s\sqrt{\log B},\qquad cB^{-2\varepsilon_0}\delta\le\frac{\nu(J)}{\|J\|} \le B^{\varepsilon_0}\delta.$$ There are fixed constants $C_*,c_*>0$, independent of $\varepsilon_0$, such that $$\label{pk:reverse-fraction} \nu\left(\left\{y\in J: \frac{\nu(R)}{\|R\|}\le B^{C_*}\delta \text{ for every dyadic }R\subset J\text{ containing }y \right\}\right) \ge B^{-c_*\varepsilon_0}\nu(J).$$ Here $\|J\|$ denotes coordinate Lebesgue volume. The metric volume is uniformly comparable to it.
  - Explanation: At each witness a dyadic cube of comparable density carries a quantitative fraction of excess mass where descendant densities stay below a threshold.
- **claim-dyadic-packing** (bound, [source](#stmt-pk-packing)): Let $\nu$ be a finite positive measure on the union $X$ of disjoint roots of a dyadic forest. For a cube $R$ put $d(R)=\nu(R)/\|R\|$. Suppose that a family of marked cubes has densities in $[\lambda_-,\lambda_+]$, where $0<\lambda_-\le\lambda_+<\infty$, and that every marked cube $P$ satisfies $$\label{pk:abstract-payment} \nu\{x\in P:d(R)\le Ld(P) \text{ for every descendant }R\ni x\} \ge\zeta\nu(P),$$ where $L\ge1$ and $0<\zeta<1$. Let $E_N$ be the union of all full cubes $F$ admitting a marked chain $$W_1\supsetneqq W_2\supsetneqq\cdots\supsetneqq W_N=F, \qquad d(W_{j+1})\ge S d(W_j).$$ For $R_*>e$ and $S>LR_*$ one has $$\label{pk:abstract-packing} \nu(E_N)\le \left[(1-\zeta)^{N-1} +\frac{e}{R_*}\left(1+ \left\lfloor\log\frac{\lambda_+}{\lambda_-}\right\rfloor \right)\right]\nu(X).$$ In particular, the full innermost witness cubes furnished by [pk:witnesses](#pk:witnesses), [pk:reverse](#pk:reverse), for a fixed band and grid, have union $E$ satisfying $$\label{pk:packed-witnesses} \nu(E)\le C\exp(-B^{1/8})d$$ when $\varepsilon_0$ is sufficiently small.
  - Explanation: Repeated density retracing along marked chains forces the union of full innermost cubes to carry exponentially little mass.
- **claim-contact-charging** (bound, [source](#stmt-pk-contact-charge)): Let $h$ and $\nu$ be finite positive measures on $\mathbb R^m$, and let $\mathcal C$ be a set of contact centers. For each of its contacts suppose there are radii $0<u_x\le s_x$ with $$\label{pk:charge-hypotheses} \nu_{s_x}(x)\ge c\theta,\qquad \nu_{\sqrt2s_x}(x)\le C\theta,\qquad h_{u_x}(x)\le C\theta,$$ where $\theta>0$ and the constants are fixed. There is a fixed $L_0$ such that, if $B_{L_0s_x}(x)\subset E$ for every contact, then $$\label{pk:charge-bound} h^*(\mathcal C)\le C\nu(E).$$ Here $E$ is Borel and $h^*$ is outer measure. The statement also holds for a submeasure of a contact measure whose spatial projection is dominated by $h$.
  - Explanation: Contacts whose threshold balls lie in a Borel set have outer contact mass bounded by a constant times the mass of that set.
- **claim-minimal-fits** (construction, [source](#stmt-ce-fit)): Fix a finite derivative order $J\ge6$. There are an integer $k_0>J+4$, integers $q,N$, and constants $C,m_*>0$, depending only on the dimensions, $Q$, and these fixed orders, with the following property. For $0<m_0<m_*$ and sufficiently small initial height and mass error, one can make the following construction simultaneously for every $x\in B_5$.* *At the scales $\ell_j=2^{-j}$ construct smooth minimal graphs $S_{x,j}=\operatorname{graph}f_{x,j}$ over $B_{80\ell_j}(x)$, and stop at the first scale for which $$\label{ce:accept} \sup\bigl\{\|X_v-f_{x,j}(X_h)\|: X\in\mathop{\mathrm{spt}}\mu,\ X_h\in B_{64\ell_j}(x)\bigr\} \le m_0\ell_j^{k_0}$$ fails. If there is no failure, all scales are constructed. The graph at the first failed scale is included in the construction. On fixed interior enlargements of its test ball, every constructed graph satisfies the height upper bound $C m_0\ell_j^{k_0}$, the tilt and mass-defect bounds $$\label{ce:fitenergy} \ell_j^{-m}\bigl(D+\|M\|\bigr)(B_{70\ell_j}(x)) \le C a_j^2, \qquad a_j=m_0\ell_j^{k_0-1},$$ and the following centering estimate. For each smooth vector test $\zeta$ supported in $B_{70}$, $$\label{ce:moment} \left\|\ell_j^{-m}\int \frac{X_v-f_{x,j}(X_h)}{\ell_j}\cdot \zeta\left(\frac{X_h-x}{\ell_j}\right)\,\mathop{}\!d\mu(X)\right\| \le C a_j^2\\|\zeta\\|_{H^q}.$$ The same assertion holds in normal coordinates over $S_{x,j}$, with base volume in the definition of the distribution.* *Every graph has physical derivatives of orders $1,\ldots,J$ bounded by $Cm_0$ in the fixed initial coordinates. If two constructed fits have comparable scales and a common interior region containing a ball of radius comparable to $\ell$, then, on a fixed smaller ball in that region, $$\label{ce:overlap} \\|D^i(f-f')\\|_{L^\infty} \le C m_0^2\ell^{2k_0-1-i},\qquad 0\le i\le J.$$ The constants in this last assertion can depend on the fixed scale ratio and the fixed interior room. No measurability of $x\mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}% \mapsto \EndAccSupp{}}f_{x,j}$ is required.
  - Explanation: Smooth minimal graphs are constructed at dyadic scales with height, tilt and centering bounds, and neighboring fits agree to second order.
- **claim-failed-fit-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ce-failed)): Fix $0<c_0<1/10$ and a tilt threshold $\varepsilon_0>0$. By reducing the admissible upper bound for $m_0$ in [ce:fit](#ce:fit), there is a constant $c>0$ with the following property. If the first failure at $x$ has scale $\ell$, then in every base ball of radius $c_0\ell$ whose center lies in $B_{30\ell}(x)$, normal projection onto the failed fit has a subset of base volume at least $c\ell^m$ on which the total integer count is $Q$ and two entries have separation at least $c m_0\ell^{k_0}$. Those two entries have tilt at most $\varepsilon_0$ relative to the fit and each has multiplicity strictly less than $Q$. The word “entry” refers to a point where the projection Jacobian is nonzero, with its integer multiplicity; no smoothness of a sheet is asserted. In particular no failure occurs when $Q=1$.
  - Explanation: A failed fit forces separated entries of lower multiplicity on a positive-volume set in every comparable subball.
- **claim-glued-center** (construction, [source](#stmt-ce-center)): Fix the desired output order $J\ge6$, apply [ce:fit](#ce:fit) with derivative order $J+1$, and then choose its corresponding $k_0$ and initial parameters. There is a $C^J$ graph $S$ on an interior neighborhood of $0$, with $C^J$ norm at most $Cm_0$, through $0$ and tangent to $P_0$ there, and a nonnegative Lipschitz function $t$ on the fixed base, such that $$\label{ce:geometry} \|H_S\|+\|\nabla H_S\|\le Cm_0^2t^{2k_0-4}, \qquad \|z\|\le Cm_0t^{k_0}\quad\hbox{on }\mathop{\mathrm{spt}}\mu.$$ In the first inequality $t$ is evaluated at the horizontal coordinate of the point of $S$; in the second it may be evaluated either at $\pi_0X$ or at $\pi_0\pi_SX$, with a change of constant. Writing $\mathcal I=\{t>0\}$, one has $$\label{ce:influence} t(0)=0,\qquad \sup_{B_r}t=o(r),\qquad \|\mathcal I\cap B_r\|=o(r^m).$$ In particular the support height is zero off the influence region. On $S$ itself its normal-projection version has the same vanishing volume density. If $\rho$ is distance to $0$ on $S$, then $\|z\|\le C\rho^2$ on the support in a sufficiently small tube.
  - Explanation: Gluing fits gives a smooth graph through the origin with curvature and support height controlled by a scale function whose influence region has vanishing density.
- **claim-weighted-height-bounds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ce-weights)): Either the support lies on $S$ in a neighborhood of $0$, or there exist $r_0>0$, $c,C>0$, and a finite $J_0$ such that, for every $0<r<r_0$, $$\label{ce:height} 0<\delta(r)\le Cr, \qquad c\delta(r)^{J_0}\le\frac{H(r)}{r^2} \le C\delta(r)^2.$$ In the latter alternative, throughout $\rho<r$ one also has $$\label{ce:pointwisecurvature} \|H_S\|+\|\nabla H_S\|\le C\delta(r)r,$$ and $$\label{ce:curvature} r^{-m}\int\phi_r\bigl(\|H_S\|^2+\|\nabla H_S\|^2\bigr)\,\mathop{}\!d\mu \le C r^{-m}\int\phi_r\|z\|^2\,\mathop{}\!d\mu.$$ The constants may depend on the fixed center construction. One may take any fixed $J_0\ge2+(m+p-1)/k_0$ after changing $c$.
  - Explanation: Either the support lies on the center near the origin or the weighted height is bounded above and below by powers of the sup height.
- **claim-constancy-regularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ce-constancy)): If the support lies on the center $S$ in a neighborhood of the origin, then the origin is regular.
  - Explanation: If the support lies on the center near the origin, constancy of multiplicity and minimality make the origin regular.
- **claim-collar-signed-estimate** (theorem, [source](#stmt-in-collar)): For $a\in C^1(B_r)$ put $$\\|a\\|_{1,r}=\sup_{B_r}\bigl(\|a\|+r\|\nabla_Sa\|\bigr).$$ There is a radius $r_{\mathrm c}>0$, depending only on the fixed center data, such that for every $0<r<r_{\mathrm c}$ the first inequality below holds for every $a\in C^1(B_r)$ with finite norm, and the second holds independently of $a$: $$\label{in:collarestimates} \begin{split} r^2\left\|\int^{(r)}a\phi\,\mathop{}\!dM\right\| &\leq \\|a\\|_{1,r}\bigl[C(E+r^2H)+\eta_1 R\bigr],\\ 2A&\leq E+Cg(E+H)+Cr^2H+\eta_1 R. \end{split}$$ The constants $C,C_1$ and the radius $r_{\mathrm c}$ are independent of the multiplier. For a family $a_r$, the coefficient of $R$ in the first line is $\\|a_r\\|_{1,r}\eta_1(r)$, so each use of its smallness must control this product. The second line uses $a=1$ only in its thin-part proof. The coefficient of $E$ in the second line is exactly one before the displayed small errors are added.
  - Explanation: The signed comparison holds up to the outer collar with the leading tilt coefficient preserved.
- **claim-frequency-inequalities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-in-frequency)): With a dot denoting differentiation with respect to $\log r$, the quantities in <a href="#in:variables" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="in:variables">[in:variables]</a> satisfy $$\begin{aligned} n_*&\leq\sqrt{q_*}+Cr^2+\eta n_*,\label{in:normalfrequency}\\ \left\|\frac{\dot A}{H}-(2P-n_*+q_*)\right\| &\leq C\{r\sqrt{q_*}+r^2(1+n_*)\}+\eta(n_*+q_*), \label{in:horizontalfrequency}\\ -m\leq\frac{\dot H}{H} &\leq2n_*+Cr+\eta(n_*+q_*).\label{in:heightfrequency} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: The normal, horizontal and height frequency quantities satisfy differential inequalities in logarithmic radius with small errors.
- **claim-inward-bootstrap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-in-bootstrap)): On all sufficiently small radii, $P$ is bounded above and below. For each fixed $\lambda\in(0,1)$ there are constants $C_\lambda,c_\lambda>0$ such that $$\label{in:doubling} c_\lambda H(r)\leq H(\lambda r)\leq C_\lambda H(r), \qquad \int_{\lambda r}^{r}q_*(v)\,\frac{\mathop{}\!dv}{v}\leq C_\lambda.$$ There is a sequence $r_j\downarrow0$ on which both $n_*(r_j)$ and $q_*(r_j)$ are bounded.
  - Explanation: The frequency ratio stays bounded at small radii and the height satisfies a doubling comparison.
- **claim-vanishing-height** (theorem, [source](#stmt-in-finish)): The positive-height alternative at the origin is impossible. Consequently every point satisfying the typical-point hypotheses <a href="#ce:typical" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ce:typical">[ce:typical]</a> is regular.
  - Explanation: The positive-height alternative is impossible at a typical point, so every such point is regular.

        "exact_quote": "Fix a bound for $Q$ and fixed concentric interior enlargements of a ball $B_s$. The count selects a unique integer $q\\ge0$ on most of an intermediate ball. If either a fixed smooth mass average differs from $Q$ by less than $1/4$, or $N=Q$ on a fixed majority of that ball, then $q=Q$. In that case $$\\label{eq:lt-scalar-mass}\n |M|(B_s)+\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_S\\{y\\in B_s:N(y)\\ne Q\\}\n                       \\le C(D+\\Lambda)(B_{cs}).$$ In the smooth-average condition the test is nonnegative, has reference integral one, and has fixed scaled derivative bounds. The same integer propagates along overlapping comparable balls for which these small-energy and mass bounds hold and whose overlap has a fixed positive relative volume.",
          "source_id": "PM-346.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix a bound for $Q$ and fixed concentric interior enlargements of a ball $B_s$. Suppose the normalized projected mass on the outer enlargement is bounded, and $$\\epsilon=s^{-m}(D+\\Lambda)(B_{cs})$$ is sufficiently small; $c>1$ is a fixed enlargement with room for the argument. The count selects a unique integer $q\\ge0$ on most of an intermediate ball. If either a fixed smooth mass average differs from $Q$ by less than $1/4$, or $N=Q$ on a fixed majority of that ball, then $q=Q$. In that case $$\\label{eq:lt-scalar-mass}\n |M|(B_s)+\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_S\\{y\\in B_s:N(y)\\ne Q\\}\n                       \\le C(D+\\Lambda)(B_{cs}).$$ In the smooth-average condition the test is nonnegative, has reference integral one, and has fixed scaled derivative bounds. The same integer propagates along overlapping comparable balls for which these small-energy and mass bounds hold and whose overlap has a fixed positive relative volume.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lt-config",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $Q\\ge1$, and choose a sufficiently large fixed enlargement $c>1$ depending only on the dimensions, $Q$, and the fixed coordinate and cylinder-room data. For every prescribed exceptional proportion $\\delta_0>0$ there is a threshold $d_0=d_0(\\delta_0)>0$, depending otherwise only on these fixed data, such that the following holds. Suppose $Q$ is selected on the enlarged ball $B_{cs}(x)$ and $$s^{-m}\\tau(B_{cs}(x))\\le d\\le d_0,$$ with the cylinder and interior-room conventions above. There is a configuration $\\mathcal A=\\{a_1,\\ldots,a_Q\\}$ of scaled heights such that, outside a subset of $B_s(x)$ of relative volume at most $\\delta_0$, the fiber consists of exactly $Q$ projection entries and can be matched to $\\mathcal A$ within $C_{\\delta_0}s\\sqrt d$. Configurations on comparable overlapping balls can be chosen, and any such majority configurations can be matched, within the sum of these bounds, provided the exceptional proportions are smaller than a fixed fraction of the overlap.*\n\n\n\n\n*For every fixed finite $p>2$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:lt-p-range}\n m(1/2-1/p)<1,$$ and every fixed $0<\\beta<\\min\\{1/2,1/m\\}$, this same configuration satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\left(s^{-m}\\int_{\\pi^{-1}(B_s(x))}\n       \\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(\\zeta,\\mathcal A)^p\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu\\right)^{1/p}\n       &\\le C_p s\\sqrt d,\\label{eq:lt-config-moment}\\\\\n \\sup_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{spt}}\\mu\\cap\\pi^{-1}(B_s(x))}\n       \\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(\\zeta,\\mathcal A)&\\le C_\\beta s d^\\beta.\n       \\label{eq:lt-config-sup}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The enlargement $c$, threshold $d_0$, and configuration do not depend on $p$ or $\\beta$. The constants $C_p,C_\\beta$ may depend on their indicated exponents and the fixed data, but not on $\\delta_0$; the majority constant $C_{\\delta_0}$ may depend on it. The support estimate holds for all support points, including points with zero projection Jacobian.",
        "source_id": "PM-346.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lt-weightedballs",
        "exact_quote": "Define $$G_s(y)=(2\\pi s^2)^{-m/2}\\exp(-|y|^2/(2s^2))$$ and fix a center $x$. On the region $|y-x|\\le Rs$, cover the base by balls of radii comparable to $s/(1+|y-x|/s)$, with fixed enlarged balls having bounded overlap. Suppose each enlarged ball meets the hypotheses of [lt:scalar](#lt:scalar) with the same propagated integer $Q$. Then $$\\label{eq:lt-weighted-scalar}\n \\int_{|y-x|\\le Rs}G_s(y-x)\\,\\mathop{}\\!d|M|(y)\n \\le C\\int_{|y-x|\\le (R+C)s}G_s(y-x)\\,\\mathop{}\\!d(D+\\Lambda)(y).$$ The constant is independent of $R$. The part outside the indicated region is retained as a tail error.*\n\n\n*For the configuration conclusion, fix one exceptional proportion $\\delta_{\\rm cmp}>0$ smaller than the fixed comparison-overlap proportions. If, in addition, on all admissible dilations $1\\le t\\le R_*$ one has $$\\label{eq:lt-polynomial-dilation}\n (ts)^{-m}\\tau(B_{cts}(x))\\le C t^Jd,$$ with $Ct^Jd\\le d_0(\\delta_{\\rm cmp})$ throughout this range and the same integer propagated, then configurations matched from the scale $s$ have moment and support estimates on a dilation $t$ equal to those in [lt:config](#lt:config), with an additional fixed power of $t$. Gaussian integrals of these estimates have constants depending on $J$ and the fixed moment order, once the tail beyond $R_*$ is accounted for.",
        "source_id": "PM-346.2"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-lt-weightedballs",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lt-gaussian",
        "exact_quote": "Set $u=(y-x)/s$, $A(s)=s^2M_s(x)$, and $$R_s(x)=\\int G_s(y-x)B[u,u]\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu.$$ In flat coordinates, apart from $\\mathcal T_s$, $$\\label{eq:lt-gaussian-flat}\n \\dot A=s^2L_s+s^2R_s,\n \\qquad \\dot{}=\\frac{\\mathop{}\\!d}{\\mathop{}\\!d\\log s}.$$ In the curved coordinates fixed above, $$\\label{eq:lt-gaussian-curved}\n \\dot A=s^2L_s+s^2R_s+\\mathcal E_s+\\mathcal T_s,\n \\qquad\n |\\mathcal E_s|\\le C\\gamma s^2\n           \\int G_s(y-x)(1+|u|^2)\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\tau(y).$$ Here the entries of $u$ in $B[u,u]$ are taken in the chosen orthonormal horizontal frame, and $\\gamma$ retains its definition from the fixed physical coordinates.",
        "source_id": "PM-346.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lt-gaussian",
          "exact_quote": "Set $u=(y-x)/s$, $A(s)=s^2M_s(x)$, and $$R_s(x)=\\int G_s(y-x)B[u,u]\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu.$$ In flat coordinates, apart from $\\mathcal T_s$, $$\\label{eq:lt-gaussian-flat}\n \\dot A=s^2L_s+s^2R_s,\n \\qquad \\dot{}=\\frac{\\mathop{}\\!d}{\\mathop{}\\!d\\log s}.$$ In the curved coordinates fixed above, $$\\label{eq:lt-gaussian-curved}\n \\dot A=s^2L_s+s^2R_s+\\mathcal E_s+\\mathcal T_s,\n \\qquad\n |\\mathcal E_s|\\le C\\gamma s^2\n           \\int G_s(y-x)(1+|u|^2)\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\tau(y).$$ Here the entries of $u$ in $B[u,u]$ are taken in the chosen orthonormal horizontal frame, and $\\gamma$ retains its definition from the fixed physical coordinates.",
          "source_id": "PM-346.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Set $u=(y-x)/s$, $A(s)=s^2M_s(x)$, and $$R_s(x)=\\int G_s(y-x)B[u,u]\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu.$$ In flat coordinates, apart from $\\mathcal T_s$, $$\\label{eq:lt-gaussian-flat}\n \\dot A=s^2L_s+s^2R_s,\n \\qquad \\dot{}=\\frac{\\mathop{}\\!d}{\\mathop{}\\!d\\log s}.$$ In the curved coordinates fixed above, $$\\label{eq:lt-gaussian-curved}\n \\dot A=s^2L_s+s^2R_s+\\mathcal E_s+\\mathcal T_s,\n \\qquad\n |\\mathcal E_s|\\le C\\gamma s^2\n           \\int G_s(y-x)(1+|u|^2)\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\tau(y).$$ Here the entries of $u$ in $B[u,u]$ are taken in the chosen orthonormal horizontal frame, and $\\gamma$ retains its definition from the fixed physical coordinates.",

      "id": "claim-gaussian-mass-identity",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [

          "exact_quote": "In flat coordinates, apart from $\\mathcal T_s$,",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lt-heighttests",
        "exact_quote": "In flat coordinates, for a base test $\\varphi$, a scalar height function $f$, a horizontal field $X$, and a normal vector function $v$, stationarity gives $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\int f\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}X\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu\n &=\\int f B:DX\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu-\n                   \\int Df\\cdot P_{vh}X\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu,\n                     \\label{eq:lt-height-horizontal}\\\\\n 0&=\\int\\{\\varphi Dv:P_{vv}\n                +v\\cdot P_{vh}\\nabla\\varphi\\}\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu.\n                     \\label{eq:lt-height-normal}\n\\end{aligned}$$ All height functions here use the scaled variable $\\zeta$. For a scale-independent $C^2$ height function $\\Gamma$, these formulas give $$\\label{eq:lt-height-gaussian}\n \\frac{\\mathop{}\\!d}{\\mathop{}\\!d\\log s}\\int G_s\\Gamma\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu\n =\\int G_s\\Gamma(B[u,u]-e)\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu\n                       +s^2\\int G_sD^2\\Gamma:P_{vv}\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu,$$ apart from the specified chart tails. If $\\Gamma$ depends on $s$, add $\\int G_s\\dot\\Gamma\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu$.*\n\n\n\n*In the curved tube, use the original radial-gauge normal frame fixed above. The extra trace terms in a normal test $\\varphi v(\\zeta)$ without a derivative of $\\varphi$ are bounded pointwise by $C|\\varphi|$ times $$\\label{eq:lt-normal-error}\n |Dv|l|z_{\\rm phys}|\\sqrt e\n       +l|v|\\bigl(|H_S|+|z_{\\rm phys}|+e+\\sqrt e\\bigr).$$ The terms with a derivative of $\\varphi$ have the flat tensor expression in <a href=\"#eq:lt-height-normal\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lt-height-normal\">[eq:lt-height-normal]</a>, with a coefficient error at most $C\\gamma|P_{vh}|$. The curved horizontal identity is covariant, with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_SX$ and $\\nabla^SX$ in place of the flat base operators. Its geometric error is bounded by the right side of <a href=\"#eq:lt-scaledvariation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lt-scaledvariation\">[eq:lt-scaledvariation]</a> with the integrand multiplied by $|f|$. The additional flux error from the height gradient is bounded pointwise by $$\\label{eq:lt-horizontal-height-error}\n C\\gamma |Df||X|(\\sqrt e+\\sqrt\\lambda).$$ In its coordinate form the divergence comparison also contributes an error bounded by $Cl\\int|f||X|\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu$; the coordinate changes in the $B$ term are included in the preceding geometric error. Under a further interior rescaling, retain the original horizontal and normal frames and the original $\\gamma$ in <a href=\"#eq:lt-small-geometry\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lt-small-geometry\">[eq:lt-small-geometry]</a>. Transform the displayed estimates by change of variables. The inherited physical connection remains $O(l)$; no improvement to $O(ls)$ is asserted on an off-center ball of coordinate radius $s$. In particular first-order flux errors have size at most $C\\gamma(\\sqrt e+\\sqrt\\lambda)$ times the relevant multiplier, whereas quadratic errors have size at most $C\\gamma(e+\\lambda)$. The factor $|v|$ in <a href=\"#eq:lt-normal-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lt-normal-error\">[eq:lt-normal-error]</a> must be retained when estimating a normal test.",
        "source_id": "PM-346.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lt-heighttests",
          "exact_quote": "In flat coordinates, for a base test $\\varphi$, a scalar height function $f$, a horizontal field $X$, and a normal vector function $v$, stationarity gives $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\int f\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}X\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu\n &=\\int f B:DX\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu-\n                   \\int Df\\cdot P_{vh}X\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu,\n                     \\label{eq:lt-height-horizontal}\\\\\n 0&=\\int\\{\\varphi Dv:P_{vv}\n                +v\\cdot P_{vh}\\nabla\\varphi\\}\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu.\n                     \\label{eq:lt-height-normal}\n\\end{aligned}$$ All height functions here use the scaled variable $\\zeta$. For a scale-independent $C^2$ height function $\\Gamma$, these formulas give $$\\label{eq:lt-height-gaussian}\n \\frac{\\mathop{}\\!d}{\\mathop{}\\!d\\log s}\\int G_s\\Gamma\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu\n =\\int G_s\\Gamma(B[u,u]-e)\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu\n                       +s^2\\int G_sD^2\\Gamma:P_{vv}\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu,$$ apart from the specified chart tails. If $\\Gamma$ depends on $s$, add $\\int G_s\\dot\\Gamma\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu$.*\n\n\n\n*In the curved tube, use the original radial-gauge normal frame fixed above. The extra trace terms in a normal test $\\varphi v(\\zeta)$ without a derivative of $\\varphi$ are bounded pointwise by $C|\\varphi|$ times $$\\label{eq:lt-normal-error}\n |Dv|l|z_{\\rm phys}|\\sqrt e\n       +l|v|\\bigl(|H_S|+|z_{\\rm phys}|+e+\\sqrt e\\bigr).$$ The terms with a derivative of $\\varphi$ have the flat tensor expression in <a href=\"#eq:lt-height-normal\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lt-height-normal\">[eq:lt-height-normal]</a>, with a coefficient error at most $C\\gamma|P_{vh}|$. The curved horizontal identity is covariant, with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_SX$ and $\\nabla^SX$ in place of the flat base operators. Its geometric error is bounded by the right side of <a href=\"#eq:lt-scaledvariation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lt-scaledvariation\">[eq:lt-scaledvariation]</a> with the integrand multiplied by $|f|$. The additional flux error from the height gradient is bounded pointwise by $$\\label{eq:lt-horizontal-height-error}\n C\\gamma |Df||X|(\\sqrt e+\\sqrt\\lambda).$$ In its coordinate form the divergence comparison also contributes an error bounded by $Cl\\int|f||X|\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu$; the coordinate changes in the $B$ term are included in the preceding geometric error. Under a further interior rescaling, retain the original horizontal and normal frames and the original $\\gamma$ in <a href=\"#eq:lt-small-geometry\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lt-small-geometry\">[eq:lt-small-geometry]</a>. Transform the displayed estimates by change of variables. The inherited physical connection remains $O(l)$; no improvement to $O(ls)$ is asserted on an off-center ball of coordinate radius $s$. In particular first-order flux errors have size at most $C\\gamma(\\sqrt e+\\sqrt\\lambda)$ times the relevant multiplier, whereas quadratic errors have size at most $C\\gamma(e+\\lambda)$. The factor $|v|$ in <a href=\"#eq:lt-normal-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lt-normal-error\">[eq:lt-normal-error]</a> must be retained when estimating a normal test.",

        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "In flat coordinates, for a base test $\\varphi$, a scalar height function $f$, a horizontal field $X$, and a normal vector function $v$, stationarity gives $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\int f\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}X\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu\n &=\\int f B:DX\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu-\n                   \\int Df\\cdot P_{vh}X\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu,\n                     \\label{eq:lt-height-horizontal}\\\\\n 0&=\\int\\{\\varphi Dv:P_{vv}\n                +v\\cdot P_{vh}\\nabla\\varphi\\}\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu.\n                     \\label{eq:lt-height-normal}\n\\end{aligned}$$ All height functions here use the scaled variable $\\zeta$. For a scale-independent $C^2$ height function $\\Gamma$, these formulas give $$\\label{eq:lt-height-gaussian}\n \\frac{\\mathop{}\\!d}{\\mathop{}\\!d\\log s}\\int G_s\\Gamma\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu\n =\\int G_s\\Gamma(B[u,u]-e)\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu\n                       +s^2\\int G_sD^2\\Gamma:P_{vv}\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu,$$ apart from the specified chart tails. If $\\Gamma$ depends on $s$, add $\\int G_s\\dot\\Gamma\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu$.*\n\n\n\n*In the curved tube, use the original radial-gauge normal frame fixed above. The extra trace terms in a normal test $\\varphi v(\\zeta)$ without a derivative of $\\varphi$ are bounded pointwise by $C|\\varphi|$ times $$\\label{eq:lt-normal-error}\n |Dv|l|z_{\\rm phys}|\\sqrt e\n       +l|v|\\bigl(|H_S|+|z_{\\rm phys}|+e+\\sqrt e\\bigr).$$ The terms with a derivative of $\\varphi$ have the flat tensor expression in <a href=\"#eq:lt-height-normal\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lt-height-normal\">[eq:lt-height-normal]</a>, with a coefficient error at most $C\\gamma|P_{vh}|$. The curved horizontal identity is covariant, with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_SX$ and $\\nabla^SX$ in place of the flat base operators. Its geometric error is bounded by the right side of <a href=\"#eq:lt-scaledvariation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lt-scaledvariation\">[eq:lt-scaledvariation]</a> with the integrand multiplied by $|f|$. The additional flux error from the height gradient is bounded pointwise by $$\\label{eq:lt-horizontal-height-error}\n C\\gamma |Df||X|(\\sqrt e+\\sqrt\\lambda).$$ In its coordinate form the divergence comparison also contributes an error bounded by $Cl\\int|f||X|\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu$; the coordinate changes in the $B$ term are included in the preceding geometric error. Under a further interior rescaling, retain the original horizontal and normal frames and the original $\\gamma$ in <a href=\"#eq:lt-small-geometry\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lt-small-geometry\">[eq:lt-small-geometry]</a>. Transform the displayed estimates by change of variables. The inherited physical connection remains $O(l)$; no improvement to $O(ls)$ is asserted on an off-center ball of coordinate radius $s$. In particular first-order flux errors have size at most $C\\gamma(\\sqrt e+\\sqrt\\lambda)$ times the relevant multiplier, whereas quadratic errors have size at most $C\\gamma(e+\\lambda)$. The factor $|v|$ in <a href=\"#eq:lt-normal-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lt-normal-error\">[eq:lt-normal-error]</a> must be retained when estimating a normal test.",
      "explanation": "Stationarity tested with height-dependent fields gives identities for height integrals and their Gaussian scale derivatives, with geometric error bounds.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

        "exact_quote": "Fix a positive integer $Q$ and $\\kappa>0$. Work in the interior normal-cylinder setting of [sec:local](#sec:local), with base coordinates scaled by a physical radius $l$ so that $B_4$ is available. Suppose $$\\label{eq:signed-smallness}\n \\tau(B_4)\\leq d=e^{-T},\n \\qquad l+\\sup|z_{\\mathrm{phys}}|\\leq e^{-\\kappa T}.$$ The supremum is over the restricted support above the available chart. The statement also holds after an interior rescaling in the inherited frames of [lt:heighttests](#lt:heighttests), provided in the curved case the second smallness condition is imposed on the inherited parameter $\\gamma$ from <a href=\"#eq:lt-small-geometry\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lt-small-geometry\">[eq:lt-small-geometry]</a>. There are a constant $C_Q$ and a threshold $T_0$, depending only on the stated fixed data, such that for $T\\geq T_0$, $$\\label{eq:signed-conclusion}\n \\int w\\,(L-C_Q\\Lambda)\\leq e^{-F_Q(T)}d$$ whenever $w$ is supported in $B_1$, $0\\leq w\\leq1$, and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}w\\leq1$. For a flat reference plane, take $\\Lambda=0$ and omit the second condition in <a href=\"#eq:signed-smallness\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:signed-smallness\">[eq:signed-smallness]</a>; constant sheet offsets need not be bounded.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ct-contact",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f$ be a finite signed Borel measure on $X$, with $M_f=f(X)>0$, and let $K$ be the entrance boundary of a continuation set. There is a probability measure $\\sigma$ on $K$ such that the positive spatial measure $$h=M_f(\\pi_X)_\\#\\sigma$$ has mass $M_f$ and, at every $(x,s_0)\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{spt}}\\sigma$, $$\\label{ct:contact-signs}\n (f-h)_{s_0}(x)\\ge0,\n \\qquad\n \\int_{s_0}^{s}v^2(f-h)_v(x)\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\log v\\ge0\n \\quad(s\\ge s_0).$$ The second inequality concerns the integral; it does not assert a pointwise sign at scales larger than $s_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-346.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-ct-localization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $|L'|\\le C_0\\tau$, where $\\tau$ is a finite positive measure on a torus of diameter comparable to $R$, and let $0\\le w\\le1$, $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}w\\le R^{-1}$. Put $$\\nu=\\tau+d\\,\\mathop{}\\!dy,\\qquad f=wL'-\\eta\\nu,\n \\qquad 0<\\eta<1.$$ Suppose, for some fixed $C_1$, that $\\tau(X)/(dR^m)\\le e^{C_1T}$. For all sufficiently large $T$, if $$\\label{ct:localization-scale}\n 0<\\rho\\le c\\eta^2R/T^2,$$ then, uniformly for every $x\\in X$ and $0<s\\le\\rho$, $$\\label{ct:localized-source}\n f_s(x)\\le w(x)L'_s(x)-\\frac\\eta2\\bigl(\\tau_s(x)+d\\bigr).$$ Here $c>0$ depends only on $C_0,C_1$ and the fixed torus shape. If $\\sigma,h$ are furnished by [ct:contact](#ct:contact) with contacts at scales $s_0\\le\\rho$, then $w(x)>0$ at each contact, and $$\\begin{aligned}\n L'_{s_0}(x)&\\ge h_{s_0}(x)+\\tfrac\\eta2\\nu_{s_0}(x),\n \\label{ct:localized-initial}\\\\\n \\int_{s_0}^s v^2\\bigl(L'-\\tfrac\\eta2\\nu\\bigr)_v(x)\n \\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\log v&\\ge0\\qquad(s_0\\le s\\le\\rho).\n \\label{ct:localized-cumulative}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, contacts lie in the interior region containing $\\{w>0\\}$. These conclusions are uniform as the bottom scale tends to zero.",

        "exact_quote": "Fix $Q>1$ and assume [sx:signed](#sx:signed) for every integer $1\\le q<Q$. For each fixed $P>0$ there are constants $C_Q,C_S$ and a function $b=b_Q$ satisfying $$\\label{ct:b-choice}\n b(T)\\gg(\\log T)^{200},\\qquad b(T)=T^{o(1)},\\qquad\n F_{Q-1}(b(T)/2)\\ge(\\log T)^3$$ such that the following holds. Under the hypotheses of [sx:signed](#sx:signed), let $\\mathcal A=\\{a_1,\\ldots,a_Q\\}$ be any majority configuration on $B_{r_{\\rm maj}}$ with local upper bound $d_{\\rm loc}=r_{\\rm maj}^{-m}d$, and suppose it has a partition into at least two nonempty groups separated by $10S(T)\\sqrt d$, where $$S(T)=e^{C_Sb(T)},\\qquad c(T)=\\frac{(\\log T)^2}{T}.$$ Let $D_{\\rm far}$ be the projection of tilt restricted to $\\{\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(z,\\mathcal A)>S(T)\\sqrt d\\}$. Then every nonnegative weight $w\\le1$, supported in $B_1$ with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}w\\le1$, satisfies $$\\label{ct:separated-estimate}\n \\int w\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\bigl(L-C_Q\\Lambda+c(T)D_{\\rm far}\\bigr)\n \\le T^{-P}d.$$ The statement holds on other fixed interior balls after rescaling. Polynomial factors in the Lipschitz norm or the enlargement of a Gaussian working ball can be allowed by increasing $P,C_S$ and the fixed constants.",

        "exact_quote": "Set $$I(s)=\\max\\left\\{\n   \\sup_{s\\le v\\le\\rho}(s/v)^K A(v),\\,\n   (s/\\rho)^K\\rho^2d\\rho^{-2m-4}\\right\\},\n \\qquad a(s)=\\log\\frac{s^2}{I(s)}.\n \\label{ex:future}$$ Starting at a threshold scale $s_0$, stop at the first scale $s_*$ with $a(s_*)=T/2$. Almost everywhere they satisfy $$\\begin{cases}\n I=A,\\quad \\dot I=\\dot A,&\\text{on the reset set }\\{I=A\\},\\\\\n \\dot I=KI,&\\text{on its complement},\n \\end{cases}\n \\qquad\n |\\dot a|+\\dfrac{|\\dot I|+|\\dot Z|}{I}\\le C.\n \\label{ex:derivatives}$$ Moreover $\\dot Z/I$ is locally Lipschitz, with derivative bounded by $C$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ex-initialization",
        "exact_quote": "At $s_0$ one can choose at most $Q$ wells and a small parameter $k_0>0$ so that the barrier of [ex:barrier](#ex:barrier) holds initially. If more than one well is used, one may arrange, for any fixed $P$, $$Z(s_0)\\le a_0^{-P}I(s_0),\\qquad U(s_0)\\le CI(s_0),$$ before any immediate mergers, with $k_0$ a small fixed constant. Alternatively there is one common well, with $$U(s_0)\\le CS_h(a_0)^2 I(s_0),\\qquad\n I(s_0)-Z(s_0)\\ge cS_h(a_0)^{-2}I(s_0),\n \\qquad k_0=cS_h(a_0)^{-6}.$$ For $Q=1$, $k_0$ is a small fixed constant.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ex-barrier",
        "exact_quote": "Along every path in [ex:envelope](#ex:envelope), the wells can be chosen piecewise constantly, with at most $Q-1$ mergers. There is a positive piecewise smooth parameter $k$, bounded above by a small fixed constant and satisfying between mergers $$\\frac{k_a}{k}=q=-\\frac{k}{a^2},\n \\label{ex:k-evolution}$$ such that the comparison <a href=\"#ex:comparison\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ex:comparison\">[ex:comparison]</a> holds. Here $C_1$ is fixed and sufficiently large. Uniformly along the path, $\\log(1/k)=o(a_0)$ as $T\\to\\infty$. Before any hypothetical first failure of the barrier, the following estimates hold, with $\\mathcal E\\le a^{-P_0/2}$: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\dot U&\\le2s^2D_s+Cs^2D_{\\mathrm{tr}}+\\mathcal E I,\n \\label{ex:moment-derivative}\\\\\n s^2R_s&\\ge4k(s^2D_s-Cs^2D_{\\mathrm{tr}})\n             -4k^2U-\\mathcal E kI.\n \\label{ex:radial-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ With more than one well, in addition, $$\\dot Z+c_2\\frac{(\\log a)^2}{a}s^2D_{\\mathrm{tr}}\n       \\le a^{-P_0}I.\n \\label{ex:transition}$$ The transition terms are absent with one well. By increasing the fixed exponents in the construction, $\\mathcal E$ can be smaller than any prescribed inverse power of $a$.",
        "source_id": "PM-346.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ex-bands",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\varepsilon_0>0$, sufficiently small for the later packing argument, and any fixed loss exponent $C_*$. For large $T$, there are $Q$ ordered disjoint bands $[B_i,2B_i]\\subset(2a_0,T/2)$, independent of the contact, such that $$B_1^{1/10}\\gg F_Q(T)^3,\\qquad\n S_h(a_0)^{C_*}\\ll B_1^{\\varepsilon_0},\\qquad\n S_1(2B_i)^{C_*}\\ll B_{i+1}^{\\varepsilon_0}.\n \\label{ex:band-spacing}$$ For $Q=1$, only the first condition is needed, and a single band of size comparable to $T$ suffices. Every contact path has a traversal contained in one band, from $a=B$ to $a=2B$, which contains no merger and on which $k\\ge B^{-\\varepsilon_0}$. The estimates <a href=\"#ex:bank\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ex:bank\">[ex:bank]</a>, <a href=\"#ex:dilations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ex:dilations\">[ex:dilations]</a>, <a href=\"#ex:derivatives\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ex:derivatives\">[ex:derivatives]</a>, <a href=\"#ex:weighted-scalar\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ex:weighted-scalar\">[ex:weighted-scalar]</a>, and <a href=\"#ex:comparison\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ex:comparison\">[ex:comparison]</a>–<a href=\"#ex:transition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ex:transition\">[ex:transition]</a> hold on that traversal. If it has several wells, the bound $\\dot Z\\le a^{-P_0}I$ holds on every preceding stage with several wells as well, and the initial value of $Z$ is the arbitrarily small quantity in [ex:initialization](#ex:initialization).",

      },

        "exact_quote": "Consider a contact path and a merger-free traversal of $[B,2B]$ provided by [ex:bands](#ex:bands), with $k\\ge B^{-\\varepsilon_0}$ throughout the traversal. Given a sufficiently small fixed number $\\vartheta>0$, there are at least $cB^{1/2}$ scales $s_j$, ordered in outward time, whose levels satisfy $$\\label{pk:level-spacing}\n B+1<a(s_j)<2B-1,\\qquad\n a(s_{j+1})-a(s_j)\\ge B^{1/3}.$$ If more than one well remains, these scales satisfy $$\\label{pk:multi-witness}\n D_{s_j}\\ge c e^{-a(s_j)},\\qquad\n D_{\\mathrm{tr}}(s_j)\\le\\vartheta e^{-a(s_j)}.$$ If only one well remains, they satisfy $$\\label{pk:single-witness}\n D_{s_j}\\ge B^{-\\varepsilon_0}e^{-a(s_j)}.$$ The constants are independent of the contact and of the positive bottom scale.         "exact_quote": "Fix a finite family of nested dyadic grids with the property that every sufficiently small ball is contained in a cube of one of the grids with comparable diameter.       "exact_statement": "Fix a finite family of nested dyadic grids with the property that every sufficiently small ball is contained in a cube of one of the grids with comparable diameter. The grids may be taken half-open, so their cubes form genuine partitions. At each witness of [pk:witnesses](#pk:witnesses) there is a cube $J$ of one of these grids such that, with $\\delta=e^{-a(s)}$, $$\\label{pk:cube-range}\n B_s(x)\\subset\\operatorname{int}J,\\qquad\n \\ell(J)\\le C s\\sqrt{\\log B},\\qquad\n cB^{-2\\varepsilon_0}\\delta\\le\\frac{\\nu(J)}{|J|}\n       \\le B^{\\varepsilon_0}\\delta.$$ There are fixed constants $C_*,c_*>0$, independent of $\\varepsilon_0$, such that $$\\label{pk:reverse-fraction}\n \\nu\\left(\\left\\{y\\in J:\n    \\frac{\\nu(R)}{|R|}\\le B^{C_*}\\delta\n    \\text{ for every dyadic }R\\subset J\\text{ containing }y\n    \\right\\}\\right)\n \\ge B^{-c_*\\varepsilon_0}\\nu(J).$$ Here $|J|$ denotes coordinate Lebesgue volume. The metric volume is uniformly comparable to it.",
      "explanation": "At each witness a dyadic cube of comparable density carries a quantitative fraction of excess mass where descendant densities stay below a threshold.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pk-packing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\nu$ be a finite positive measure on the union $X$ of disjoint roots of a dyadic forest. For a cube $R$ put $d(R)=\\nu(R)/|R|$. Suppose that a family of marked cubes has densities in $[\\lambda_-,\\lambda_+]$, where $0<\\lambda_-\\le\\lambda_+<\\infty$, and that every marked cube $P$ satisfies $$\\label{pk:abstract-payment}\n \\nu\\{x\\in P:d(R)\\le Ld(P)\n       \\text{ for every descendant }R\\ni x\\}\n       \\ge\\zeta\\nu(P),$$ where $L\\ge1$ and $0<\\zeta<1$. Let $E_N$ be the union of all full cubes $F$ admitting a marked chain $$W_1\\supsetneqq W_2\\supsetneqq\\cdots\\supsetneqq W_N=F,\n \\qquad d(W_{j+1})\\ge S d(W_j).$$ For $R_*>e$ and $S>LR_*$ one has $$\\label{pk:abstract-packing}\n \\nu(E_N)\\le\n \\left[(1-\\zeta)^{N-1}\n +\\frac{e}{R_*}\\left(1+\n      \\left\\lfloor\\log\\frac{\\lambda_+}{\\lambda_-}\\right\\rfloor\n                  \\right)\\right]\\nu(X).$$ In particular, the full innermost witness cubes furnished by [pk:witnesses](#pk:witnesses), [pk:reverse](#pk:reverse), for a fixed band and grid, have union $E$ satisfying $$\\label{pk:packed-witnesses}\n \\nu(E)\\le C\\exp(-B^{1/8})d$$ when $\\varepsilon_0$ is sufficiently small.",
        "source_id": "PM-346.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        "anchor": "stmt-pk-contact-charge",
        "exact_quote": "Let $h$ and $\\nu$ be finite positive measures on $\\mathbb R^m$, and let $\\mathcal C$ be a set of contact centers. For each of its contacts suppose there are radii $0<u_x\\le s_x$ with $$\\label{pk:charge-hypotheses}\n \\nu_{s_x}(x)\\ge c\\theta,\\qquad\n \\nu_{\\sqrt2s_x}(x)\\le C\\theta,\\qquad\n h_{u_x}(x)\\le C\\theta,$$ where $\\theta>0$ and the constants are fixed. There is a fixed $L_0$ such that, if $B_{L_0s_x}(x)\\subset E$ for every contact, then $$\\label{pk:charge-bound}\n h^*(\\mathcal C)\\le C\\nu(E).$$ Here $E$ is Borel and $h^*$ is outer measure. The statement also holds for a submeasure of a contact measure whose spatial projection is dominated by $h$.",
        "source_id": "PM-346.2"
      },

There is a fixed $L_0$ such that, if $B_{L_0s_x}(x)\\subset E$ for every contact, then $$\\label{pk:charge-bound}\n h^*(\\mathcal C)\\le C\\nu(E).$$ Here $E$ is Borel and $h^*$ is outer measure.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ce-fit",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a finite derivative order $J\\ge6$. There are an integer $k_0>J+4$, integers $q,N$, and constants $C,m_*>0$, depending only on the dimensions, $Q$, and these fixed orders, with the following property. For $0<m_0<m_*$ and sufficiently small initial height and mass error, one can make the following construction simultaneously for every $x\\in B_5$.*\n\n\n\n\n*At the scales $\\ell_j=2^{-j}$ construct smooth minimal graphs $S_{x,j}=\\operatorname{graph}f_{x,j}$ over $B_{80\\ell_j}(x)$, and stop at the first scale for which $$\\label{ce:accept}\n \\sup\\bigl\\{|X_v-f_{x,j}(X_h)|:\n       X\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{spt}}\\mu,\\ X_h\\in B_{64\\ell_j}(x)\\bigr\\}\n       \\le m_0\\ell_j^{k_0}$$ fails. If there is no failure, all scales are constructed. The graph at the first failed scale is included in the construction. On fixed interior enlargements of its test ball, every constructed graph satisfies the height upper bound $C m_0\\ell_j^{k_0}$, the tilt and mass-defect bounds $$\\label{ce:fitenergy}\n \\ell_j^{-m}\\bigl(D+|M|\\bigr)(B_{70\\ell_j}(x))\n       \\le C a_j^2,\n \\qquad a_j=m_0\\ell_j^{k_0-1},$$ and the following centering estimate. For each smooth vector test $\\zeta$ supported in $B_{70}$, $$\\label{ce:moment}\n \\left|\\ell_j^{-m}\\int\n   \\frac{X_v-f_{x,j}(X_h)}{\\ell_j}\\cdot\n   \\zeta\\left(\\frac{X_h-x}{\\ell_j}\\right)\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu(X)\\right|\n       \\le C a_j^2\\|\\zeta\\|_{H^q}.$$ The same assertion holds in normal coordinates over $S_{x,j}$, with base volume in the definition of the distribution.*\n\n\n*Every graph has physical derivatives of orders $1,\\ldots,J$ bounded by $Cm_0$ in the fixed initial coordinates. If two constructed fits have comparable scales and a common interior region containing a ball of radius comparable to $\\ell$, then, on a fixed smaller ball in that region, $$\\label{ce:overlap}\n \\|D^i(f-f')\\|_{L^\\infty}\n       \\le C m_0^2\\ell^{2k_0-1-i},\\qquad 0\\le i\\le J.$$ The constants in this last assertion can depend on the fixed scale ratio and the fixed interior room. No measurability of $x\\mathrel{%\n  \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}%\n  \\mapsto\n  \\EndAccSupp{}}f_{x,j}$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-346.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ce-failed",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<c_0<1/10$ and a tilt threshold $\\varepsilon_0>0$. By reducing the admissible upper bound for $m_0$ in [ce:fit](#ce:fit), there is a constant $c>0$ with the following property. If the first failure at $x$ has scale $\\ell$, then in every base ball of radius $c_0\\ell$ whose center lies in $B_{30\\ell}(x)$, normal projection onto the failed fit has a subset of base volume at least $c\\ell^m$ on which the total integer count is $Q$ and two entries have separation at least $c m_0\\ell^{k_0}$. Those two entries have tilt at most $\\varepsilon_0$ relative to the fit and each has multiplicity strictly less than $Q$. The word “entry” refers to a point where the projection Jacobian is nonzero, with its integer multiplicity; no smoothness of a sheet is asserted. In particular no failure occurs when $Q=1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-346.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ce-center",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the desired output order $J\\ge6$, apply [ce:fit](#ce:fit) with derivative order $J+1$, and then choose its corresponding $k_0$ and initial parameters. There is a $C^J$ graph $S$ on an interior neighborhood of $0$, with $C^J$ norm at most $Cm_0$, through $0$ and tangent to $P_0$ there, and a nonnegative Lipschitz function $t$ on the fixed base, such that $$\\label{ce:geometry}\n |H_S|+|\\nabla H_S|\\le Cm_0^2t^{2k_0-4},\n \\qquad\n |z|\\le Cm_0t^{k_0}\\quad\\hbox{on }\\mathop{\\mathrm{spt}}\\mu.$$ In the first inequality $t$ is evaluated at the horizontal coordinate of the point of $S$; in the second it may be evaluated either at $\\pi_0X$ or at $\\pi_0\\pi_SX$, with a change of constant. Writing $\\mathcal I=\\{t>0\\}$, one has $$\\label{ce:influence}\n t(0)=0,\\qquad\n \\sup_{B_r}t=o(r),\\qquad\n |\\mathcal I\\cap B_r|=o(r^m).$$ In particular the support height is zero off the influence region. On $S$ itself its normal-projection version has the same vanishing volume density. If $\\rho$ is distance to $0$ on $S$, then $|z|\\le C\\rho^2$ on the support in a sufficiently small tube.",
        "source_id": "PM-346.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **regular point** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A point $p\in\mathop{\mathrm{spt}}\mu\cap U$ is *regular* if there are a neighborhood $W$ of $p$, a smooth embedded minimal $m$-submanifold $\Sigma\subset W$, and an integer $q\geq1$ such that the restriction of the varifold to $W$ is $q\|\Sigma\|$.
- **singular set** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The remaining support points form $\operatorname{Sing}V$.
- **configuration of size Q** ([source](#sec:local)): A configuration of size $Q$ is therefore an unordered list of $Q$ vectors, with repetitions allowed.
- **entrance boundary** ([source](#pm-h-15)): Its entrance boundary is $K=\mathcal D\setminus\operatorname{int}\mathcal D$, where the interior is taken in $X\times(0,\infty)$.
- **majority configuration** ([source](#ct:majority-matching)): A *majority configuration* on $B_s(x)$ is a list of $Q$ heights such that, outside a set of base volume at most $\delta_{\rm maj}\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_S(B_s(x))$, each fiber has exactly $Q$ projection entries and these entries match the list within $C_{\rm maj}s\sqrt{d_{\rm loc}}$.
- **separated partition of a list** ([source](#pm-h-17)): A partition of a list’s entries into groups is separated by $\delta$ if different groups have mutual distance at least $\delta$.
